                                                                     Ukrainian drone strikes Samara hub Nov. 2

                                                                     Pipeline shipped 7.573 mil mt in Jan-Sept

                                                                         Kazakhstan has resumed crude flows through the Atyrau-
                                                                     Samara pipeline following a one-day halt, the Kazakh energy
                                                                     ministry told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, Nov. 3.

                                                                         "As of Nov. 3, 2026, oil transportation via the Atyrau-Samara
                                                                     pipeline is proceeding as normal. Pumping operations were
                                                                     resumed following a technical inspection," the ministry said in a
                                                                     statement.

                                                                         The Samara production and dispatch hub in Russia
                                                                     came under a Ukrainian drone attack Nov. 2, after which
                                                                     oil transportation through the Atyrau-Samara pipeline was
                                                                     suspended, according to the ministry.

    Four transatlantic fixtures were reported on Nov. 3, with the
latest from Eni, which booked the Aiolos loading Nov. 15-17 for
a Dos Bocas-Mediterranean run at w590, with demurrage at
$120,000/d. Multiple market participants said that trade was the
latest trans-Atlantic fixture and was reflective of the market. The
three other fixtures, USGC-UKC runs at w650, w640 and w615,
were not deemed fresh.

    Platts assessed the benchmark 70,000 mt USGC-UKC
route down w75 to w590, in line with unanimous active market
indications. It was the benchmark's lowest assessed value since
Sept. 23.

    Benchmark rates also fell to multi-week lows on the West
Coast. Market participants reported hearing multiple tankers on
subjects for Vancouver-China voyages at rates ranging from under
$11 million to $11.75 million. However, Platts could not confirm
those details during the Nov. 3 Market on Close assessment
process.
